1. Introduction
The 3N+1 Problem concerns the behaviour of natural numbers N ∊ ℕ using these rules:
• If the the number N is Odd multiply N by 3 and add 1 (Type Odd operation)
• If the the number N is Even divide N by 2
(Type Even operation)
• Repeat with the new value (i.e. with the Result of the iteration/operation)
For example for N0 = 3 as the Initial Value: 30 ↗ 101 ↘ 52 ↗ 163 ↘ 84 ↘ 45 ↘ *26 ↘ †17
The above number-sequence is informally called an iterationseries (for the Initial Value N0)
while the order of operations Odd & Even is called the parity-sequence for the iterationseries.
The Collatz Conjecture states, that eventually any iterationseries will reach the Loop-value †1
(alternatively that iterationseries for all N0 except {1,2,4,8} reaches the "bottleneck-value" 16).
Definition 1
• Let j ∊ ℕ0 count the Total number of operations in an iterationseries (j is the index in Nj)
• Let s ∊ ℕ0 count the number of type Odd operations in an iterationseries
• Let r ∊ ℕ0 count the number of type Even operations in an iterationseries
It is observed that s+r = j

i.e. #Odd(s) + #Even(r) = #Total(j) (where # is number of pcs.)

All the iterationseries which reach the End Value Nj = †1j are called Convergent iterationseries
and it is possible to identify the iterationseries with the corresponding End †parity-sequence.
All iterationseries which reach a *Reduced Value *Nj < N0 are *Converging iterationseries and it
is possible to identify the iterationseries with the corresponding *reducing *parity-sequence.
NB! Everywhere in the present work a star/asterisk * represents that something is *reducing.
1 of 66

All iterationseries that reach Nj = †1j continues in The Loop

⋯ 1j ↗ 4j+1 ↘ 2j+2 ↘ 1j+3 ⋯

Parity-sequences can be constructed from O E -tiles & E -tiles like Domino-tiles, because for
every odd N = (2C-1), C ∊ ℕ, the Result after a type Odd operation is even 3(2C-1)+1 = (6C-2),
so the next operation is a type Even (6C-2)/2 = (3C-1). The operations together is considered a
unit named an O E -tile and the other unit for type Even operations following other type
Even operations is named an E -tile. OBS! The parity of (3C-1) ≡ ? (mod 2) depends on C ∊ ℕ.
In the above example it is observed, that the Initial Value 30 reach the End Value †17 in seven
operations of which s = 2 are type Odd and r = 5 are type Even. The End †parity-sequence is:
†

R O E - O E - E - E - E - D where R indicates a staRting point and D the EnD point.

It is also observed, that the Initial Value 30 reach the *Reduced Value *26 in six operations of
which s = 2 are type Odd and *rs = *42 are type Even. The *reducing *parity-sequence is:
* R O E - O E - E - E - T where T indicates the Total/Terminating *parity-sequence.
The E -tile indicates the last type Even operation in the *reducing *parity-sequence.
Comment
It is emphasized that the *Reduced Value *Nj is defined to be the first value
(the lowest j) for which it is true that *Nj < N0, and that the End Value †1j is
defined to be the first value (the lowest j) for which it is true that Nj = †1j.
A brief history of The Problem
The QN±D Problem, N ∊ ℕ, for Q and D odd natural numbers have been known for a long time.
The version for Q = 3 & D = 1 have puzzled mathematicians and hopeful amateurs for a more
or less equally long time. The 3N+1 Problem is these days known as The Collatz Conjecture
and is named after Lothar Collatz (1910-1990†) a German mathematician who - according to
Wikipedia[I] - worked on The Problem in the late 1930's and introduced The Problem to an
increasing number of mathematicians during the 1950's, slowly bringing it into the public eye.
The Collatz Conjecture is easy to state, but surprisingly difficult to prove. It has gained
some notoriety amongst professional mathematicians for being a "rabbit hole" that leads to a
lot of wasted time (which this author can confirm is absolutely true) but not to useful results.
Jeffrey C. Lagarias have made a comprehensive study of The Problem, collecting known
literature in "The 3x+1 Problem: An Annotated Bibliography (1963-1999) [II]". Some progress
have been made over the years and it is well known that The Conjecture holds for "Almost All"
integers; Riho Terras[III] (1976) shows that the Asymptotic Density for the Diverging paritysequences tends to zero when modulo tends to infinity in the Union of residue-classes and
very importantly that the densities (the density of a residue-class is defined to be the inverse
of the modulo i.e. 1/modulo) can be calculated using the Binomial-distribution (NB! In the
present work Formulas are shown, which produce results identical to results from R. Terras).
Axiom 1
If the conjecture is true, and all iterationseries for all N ∊ ℕ as the Initial Value N0 eventually
do reach The Loop, then it is of course also true, that the series contains a *Reduced Value *Nj.
Vice versa: If it is true for all *iterationseries for all N ∊ ℕ as the Initial Value N0 that the series
contains a *Reduced Value *Nj then it is also true, that all series eventually reach The Loop.
If all N0 > 2 reach a *Reduced Value *Nj < N0 then the *Nj is the new N0 in a new *iterationseries.
2 of 66

Axiom 2
If the conjecture is false, then it is true that there exist a lowest counterexample Nce ∊ ℕ which
have an iterationseries (with a parity-sequence) that never reach the End Value Nj = †1j and in
that case it is also true, that the iterationseries for Nce as the Initial Value N0 = Nce never reach
a *Reduced Value *Nj < Nce, because if it does, then Nce is not the lowest counterexample.
Axiom 3
For all N ∊ ℕ, N ≠ 2c, it is true that there exist a c ∊ ℕ for which it is true that: 2c-1 < N < 2c
Corollary 1
From the above Axioms follows that the hypothetical Nce have a finite size: 2r -1 < Nce < 2r
ce

ce

Introduction to the method and model
Most arguments are based on modular arithmetic, graph-theory and combinatorics.
Following the rules in The 3N+1 Problem the present work is an analysis of the behaviour of
residue-classes N ≡ P (mod 2r), P ≤ 2r, in a Covering System including all P ≤ 2r, P ∊ ℕ, r ∊ ℕ.
Different notations can be used to describe the same modular set N ≡ P (mod A), P ≤ A. In the
present work [AX-B], B < A, is used to define the set of all NX = (AX-B) ⇔ NX ≡ (A-B) (mod A).
It is demonstrated that in this model the modulo A can only take on certain values A = 2r3s.
OBS! Notice that in a class [AX-B] the independent variable X ∊ ℕ describes the position of NX.
It is demonstrated, that it is possible to perform Collatz-operations on congruence-classes
(residue-classes) resulting in Class-series for Initial Value classes N ≡ PIV (mod 2r), P ≤ 2r,
which have a finite number of terms and ends at Terminal Value classes N ≡ PTV (mod 3s). For
example the *Converging Class-series *[2*2 X-*30]IV ⥂O [2231X-81] ⥂E [2131X-42] ⥂E [31X-23]TV &
the ϕDiverging Class-series ϕ[22X-10]IV ⥂O [2231X-21] ⥂E [2131X-12] ⥂O [2132X-23] ⥂E [32X-14]TV
where conclusions can be made from the difference in the size of the modulo 2r > 3s or 2r < 3s.
NB! Everywhere in the present work the symbol ϕ represents that something is ϕDiverging.
Class-series (for IV-classes) can - like iterationseries for values N0 - be identified with a paritysequence with a finite number of tiles as all the elements in the class share the same sequence.
1

It is shown, that it is possible to perform Collatz-operations on values N ∊ ℕ in Branches for
N = (2C-1)2r i.e. r > 0 implies a Type Even operation and r = 0 implies a Type Odd operation.
A system of flowgraphs (containing all odd N) is constructed, showing Collatz-operations on
classes [2r3sX-B] and the development for changing (increasing) modulo. The flowgraphs are
used to illustrate parity-sequences (for Class-series), which are analysed using binary graphs.
For parity-sequences in the Infinite Complete Binary Trees - a (rooted) Tree is a graph having
a Root-node with a unique route (path) to any other node - the following Axiom is important:
Axiom 4
In an Infinite Complete Binary Tree all nodes in a layer/level k ∊ ℕ0 have two Child-nodes in
the next layer k+1, so all nodes in a layer k ∊ ℕ have a sub-tree identical to the sub-tree for
the Root-node in layer k = 0. All nodes in layer k are Parent-nodes (i.e. to the two Left/RightChildren in the next layer k+1). All nodes except the Root-node are Child-nodes (i.e. from the
Parent-node in layer k-1). In each layer k there exist 2k unique nodes. There exist a countable
infinity of layers for k → ∞. This implies that the Total number of nodes (i.e. of unique routes)
in the Tree is an Uncountable Infinity. When the countable infinity of layers k is included, it is
only possible to index one node in each layer with k which leaves 2k-1 nodes without index.
NB! It is well known[IV] that the power-set of a countable infinity is an Uncountable Infinity.
3 of 66

Collatz-operations on congruence-classes
Suppose a class N ≡ P (mod A) is described by three variables; N(A,X,B) = [AX-B], B = A-P.
When the modulo A is even then AX is even and this implies, that the parity of [AX-B] is then
determined only by the parity of the constant B < A and is independent of the variable X ∊ ℕ.
This implies, that when A is even it is possible to perform a Collatz-operation on the class:
•
•
•

Even A & odd Bj in [AX-Bj] ⇒ a type Odd operation: [AX-Bj] ⥂O [3AX-(3Bj-1)j+1]
Even A & even Bj in [AX-Bj] ⇒ a type Even operation: [AX-Bj] ⥂E [(A/2)X-(Bj/2)j+1]
Odd A in [AX-Bj] ⇒ No operation is possible - the parity of the class now depends on X

Notice that in a class [AX-Bj] the modulo A is the distance between neighbour-elements NX &
NX+1 = (NX+A), and P = (A-Bj) = N1 is the lowest value in the infinite set; NX ∊ {N1 ,N2 ,N3 , ... ,NX , ...}
Initial Value classes [2rX-B0]IV are defined to have modulo 2r, r ∊ ℕ0, and for the modulo A
there are two possibilities for the new modulo 3A or A/2, so all the used moduli are A = 2r3s.
r > 0 & odd Bj in [2r3sX-Bj] ⇒ a type Odd operation: [2r3sX-Bj] ⥂O [2r3s+1X-(3Bj-1)j+1]
r > 0 & even Bj in [2r3sX-Bj] ⇒ a type Even operation: [2r3sX-Bj] ⥂E [2r-13s X-(Bj/2)j+1]
r = 0 ⇒ The class is a Terminal Value class [3sX-Bj=s+r]TV and the Class-series Terminates
All classes in the used model have the form NX ∊ [2r3sX-Bj], X ∊ ℕ, 0 ≤ Bj < 2r3s , r ∊ ℕ0, s ∊ ℕ0.
NX ∊ [2030X-00]IV/TV, X ∊ ℕ ⇒ NX = X is the Universe-class (the Entire Domain) in the model.
It is not possible to perform an operation on the Universe-class, because the modulo is odd,
but when N ≡ 1 (mod 1) is split into odd values N ≡ 1 (mod 2) and even values N ≡ 2 (mod 2)
it is possible to perform operations on the two Child-classes [21X-10]IV ⋃ [21X-00]IV = [20X-00]IV:

of *Converging IV-classes *[2*rsX-*BCon]IV are named ΔRedundant IV-classes Δ[2rX-B0]IV, r > *rs.

For all X ∊ ℕ it is true that [32X-14]TV > [22X-10]IV ⇒ ϕ[22X-10]IV is a ϕDiverging IV-class.
For all [2rX-B0]IV, B0 < 2r, the Class-series can be constructed; [2rX-B0]IV → ... → [3sX-Bj=r+s]TV
4 of 66

Covering systems
In the context of the present work a Covering System is a set of congruence-classes [AX-B]
for which it is true that the Union of all the classes Covers the set of natural numbers N ∊ ℕ.
OBS! In a proper/genuine Covering System all the unique classes [AX-B] are pairwise disjoint.
NB! Notice that X ∊ ℕ but if X ∊ ℤ in the classes [AX-B], 0 ≤ B < A the System Covers all N ∊ ℤ.
Below are shown examples of different Covering Systems encountered in the model:

⋯ Notice that for constant A = 2r3s there exist 2r3s different pairs (P+B) = 2r3s, P ∊ ℕ, B ∊ ℕ0
Even though a Covering System is usually required to have a finite amount of residue-classes,
it is possible to construct Covering Systems, which have an infinite amount of residue-classes,
and there also exist Covering Systems, for which the countably infinite [2rX-B] have different
moduli 2r, r ∊ ℕ. This does not contradict the Mirsky-Newman theorem, which states that for
a finite amount of residue-classes that are both (pairwise) disjoint and distinct (have different
moduli) it is impossible for the Union of the residue-classes to Cover all N ∊ ℤ (all integers).
Comment
Appendix Alpha is a List containing the first u ≤ 142 *Converging IV-classes *u[2*rsX-*BCon]q
where the uindex u ∊ ℕ is used to identify a unique *reducing IV-class and the pair u(Pq+*BCon),
and the indexq q ∊ ℕ is used to identify the Pq-values; P1 < P2 < ... < Pq in a Group for A = 2*rs.
The List also includes the corresponding TV-classes [3sX-Bj=s+*rs]TV from the *Class-series. The
*u≤142[2*rsX-*BCon]q were found by constructing the Class-series [2rX-B0]IV → ... → [3sX-Bj=s+r]TV
for all IV-classes [2rX-B0]IV for all B0 ≡ 1 (mod 4), B0 < 2r, where modulo AIV = 2r for all r ≤ 13,
thereby confirming the *Converging IV-classes and the ϕDiverging IV-classes. All subsets of
*Converging IV-classes are ΔRedundant and ΔB0 ≡ *BCon (mod 2*rs) for all r > *rs in Δ[2rX-ΔB0]IV.
In the present work it is shown, that there exist a countable infinity of *Converging IVclasses *u→∞[2*rsX-*BCon]q →∞, u ∊ ℕ, and that the Union of all *u[2*rsX-*BCon]q for all u ∊ ℕ is in
itself a Covering System, as all the *Converging IV-classes *u[2*rsX-*BCon]q are pairwise disjoint.
It is also shown that there exist a countable infinity of ϕDiverging IV-classes ϕu'[2rX-B0]q'
u' ∊ ℕ, but very important; the ϕDiverging IV-classes are not pairwise disjoint because all the
ϕ
[2rX-B0]IV are proper subsets of a ϕDiverging ϕ[2kX-B0]IV, k < r, with higher density; 1/2 > 1/2 .
Also all *u[2*rsX-*BCon]q are proper subsets of a ϕDiverging ϕ[2kX-B0]IV, k < *rs (the proper
subsets of the *Converging IV-classes are considered ΔRedundant IV-classes Δ[2rX-ΔB0]IV, r > *rs).
NB! Also shown is the existence of an Uncountable Infinity of proper subsets of *u[2*rsX-*BCon]q,
actually an Uncountable Infinity of proper subsets of any IV-class [2rX-B0]IV in the used model.

2. The Flowcharts (flowgraphs) for Collatz-iterations
Collatz-operations on values N in Branches
Suppose N ∊ ℕ is described by the odd part (2C-1), C ∊ ℕ & even part 2r, r ∊ ℕ0 N = (2C-1)2r
For r > 0 in (2C-1)2r a Type Even operation is observed: (2C-1)2r ⥂ (2C-1)2r-1
For r = 0 in (2C-1)2r a Type Odd operation is observed: (2C-1) ⥂ 3(2C-1)+1 = (6C-2)
OBS! Notice that the Dynamics in The Problem is the result of the two above shown bijections.
A Branch (2C-1)2r is uniquely defined by the value (2C-1) and contains only Even operations.

It is observed for the Result of a Type Odd operation that: 3(2Ca-1)+1 = (6Ca-2) = (2Cb-1)2r .
The value Nb = (6Ca-2) = (2Cb-1)2r is called the connection-point for the odd value Na = (2Ca-1).
b

b

Lemma 1
For the Result of a Type Odd operation (2Cb-1)2r one of the following two possibilities is true:
b

(2Cb-1) ≡ 1 (mod 6) ∧ rb = 2n, n ∊ ℕ or

(2Cb-1) ≡ 5 (mod 6) ∧ rb = 2n-1, n ∊ ℕ

Proof for Lemma 1
For X ∊ ℕ, n ∊ ℕ
(6X-3)2n ≡ 0 (mod 6)

(6X-5)22n-1 ≡ 2 (mod 6) (6X-1)22n-1 ≡ 4 (mod 6)
(6X-5)22n ≡ 4 (mod 6) (6X-1)22n ≡ 2 (mod 6) ∎

Theorem 1
There exist one and only one simple loop where it is true that: 3(2C-1)+1 = (2C-1)2r
Proof for Theorem 1
3(2C-1)+1 = (2C-1)2r ⇔ (2C-1)2r - 3(2C-1) = 1 ⇔ (2C-1)(2r-3) = 1 ⇒ (2C-1) = 1 ∧ (2r-3) = 1.
(2C-1) = 1 ⇒ C = 1 & (2r-3) = 1 ⇒ r = 2 so in the loop it is true that: 3(2(1)-1)+1 = (2(1)-1)22 ∎
OBS! The above mentioned simple loop is The Loop ⋯ 1j ↗ 4j+1 ↘ 2j+2 ↘ 1j+3 ⋯
Corollary 2
For all Ca > 1 in (2Ca-1) it is true for 3(2Ca-1)+1 = (2Cb-1)2r that: Ca ≠ Cb ⇔ (2Ca-1) ≠ (2Cb-1).
b

Notice that Ca > 1 does not imply that Cb > 1 e.g. for Ca = 3: 3(2(3a)-1)+1 = (2Cb-1)24 ⇒ Cb = 1.
b

While a Value-Branch (2C-1)2r is valid for a particular odd (2C-1), C ∊ ℕ, r ∊ ℕ0, a Class-Branch
is valid for a particular odd class [2r3sX-Bj], Bj odd, r > 0, i.e. for all NX ∊ [2r3sX-Bj], X ∊ ℕ.

Notice that [6X-5] ⋃ [6X-3] ⋃ [6X-1] = ϕ[2X-1]IV i.e. all odd natural numbers
and that [18X-14] ⋃ [18X-8] ⋃ [18X-2] = [6X-2] i.e. all possible connection-points
For the Main-branches it is observed, that the modulo in the odd classes is A = 2131.
A branch which have a higher modulo in the odd class [2r3sX-Bj], Bj odd, for r > 1
and/or s > 1, contains a subset of one of the three different types of Main-branches.

different classes. The below illustration shows the types of nodes used in the Flowcharts.

N ≡ 6 (mod 12)

The Class-branches only contains type Even operations while type Odd operations are links
(connections) between different branches in the Flowcharts/flowgraphs. In the Flowcharts
shape and colour of the nodes reflects the congruence modulo 6. Three type even nodes are
used and one type odd node. Type of branch depends on the congruence modulo 3 for the
odd class in the branch. It is possible to colour an odd node when the congruence modulo 3
is known; (6X-5) ≡ 1 (mod 3), (6X-1) ≡ 2 (mod 3), (6X-3) ≡ 3 (mod 3). In the Initial Value
classes [2rX-Bj=0]IV the modulo does not have 3 as a factor, so it is not possible to determine the
congruence modulo 6 i.e. the IV-classes contains subsets from all the three different types.
7 of 66

All the nodes contain the Result of a Type Even operation, so it is possible to extrapolate
against type E-arrows and construct "the even previous node"; [2r3sX-Bj] ←E [2r+13sX-(2Bj)j-1]
Notice that it is possible to extrapolate "to the right" from the connections-points on top and
extrapolate "down on the page" without any limitations when extrapolating against the ←E .
For a connection-point it is possible to extrapolate against type O-arrows and construct
"the odd previous node" [6X-2j] ←O [2X-1j-1]; [2r3sX-Bj] ←O [2r3s-1X-(Bj+1/3)j-1] for r > 0.
Eventually an IV-class [2rX-Bj]IV is reached - where the congruence modulo 3 depends on X.
The nodes contain well-defined sets of values NX, and because two different descriptions of
the set are shown; NX ∊ [2r3sX-B] ⇔ NX ≡ P (mod 2r3s) where (P+B) = 2r3s, all the important
information about the set is known. The smallest value N1 in each set is P = (2r3s-B) and the
second-smallest value is N2 = (P+2r3s) = (2r+13s-B). The modulo and the constant B are known.
When the arrows are followed from node to node the Class-series describes the iterationseries for all NX-values in the classes and in particular for the P-value (for X = 1) in each class.
Comment
It is observed that the ϕDiverging IV-class ϕ[22X-1]IV is found as the first Side-branch
connecting to the first connection-point in Type 2 branches, and the IV-class ϕ[22X-1]IV is
named The Domain-class (which is a proper subset of The Universe-class) as the set
{31,72,113,154, ... ,NX, ...} contains all NX not in the Union *1[2*10X-*0Con]1 ⋃ *2[2*21X-*3Con]1.
The below "Minimal Flowcharts" demonstrates the minimum amount of extrapolation which
is required to have all odd N Covered i.e. included in at least one class [2r3sX-B] for odd B, r > 0.
The Flowcharts illustrates the earlier described Class-series [2rX-Bj=0]IV → ⋯ → [3sX-Bj=s+r]TV

NB! The density 1/2r for an IV-class [2rX-B0]IV is defined to be the inverse of the modulo 2r.
Notice that the Domain-class ϕ[22X-1]IV ⇔ N ≡ 3 (mod 4) is found in the first Side-branch
(n = 1) on the Type 2 Main-branch i.e. all the other Side-branches contains subsets of the
*Converging IV-class *NX ∊ *2[2*21X-*3Con]IV ⇔ *NX ≡ 1 (mod 4) ⇔ *NX ∊ {11 ,52 ,93 ,134 ,175 , ...}
and notice that parity-sequences for these subsets starts with the *reducing R O E - E -...
while the parity-sequence for all NX ∊ ϕ[22X-1]IV starts with the ϕDiverging R O E - O E -...
Comment
It is significant that all IV-classes in Side-branches are pairwise disjoint as for all (2C-1), C ∊ ℕ:
N = (2C-1) have a specific position X ∊ ℕ in a unique IV-class NX ∊ [2rX-B0]IV in a specific Sidebranch n ∊ ℕ from the above Lists of Side-branches n on the Type 1 branch & Type 2 branch.
Notice that all odd values in the classes [6X-5] and [6X-1] are also elements in one of the Sidebranches from the above Lists, and from Corollary 2 (p. 6) it is known, that no (2C-1) > 1 can
possibly be connected to a connection-point in "its own Branch" (2C-1)2r. This is a very strong
indication, that an alternative loop (other than the known Loop ...1↗4↘2↘1...) can not exist.
The later Discussion contains more details about why an alternative loop can not exist (NB! It
is possible to prove this after the analysis of parity-sequences in the following main-sections).
Note
In the below Flowcharts for triple modulo it is demonstrated, that it is possible to extrapolate
against an additional Type Odd operation ⥂O every time the modulo A is tripled to 3A, so it
is possible to determine the congruence modulo 3 in more of the (now coloured) odd classes.
NB! The Flowcharts for triple modulo are illustrations of the proof for the following Lemma 2.

3f. Extrapolated from N ≡ 17 (mod 2132)
TV-class is N ∊ [33X-1] ⇔ N ≡ 26 (mod 27)

Be aware that the iterationseries for 7 as the Initial Value N0 is:
70 ↗ 221 ↘ 112 ↗ 343 ↘ 174 ↗ 525 ↘ 266 ↘ 137 ↗ 408 ↘ 209 ↘ 1010 ↘ *511 ↗ 1612 ↘ 813 ↘ 414 ↘ 215 ↘ †116

Notice that all P ∊ {70c,112d,174f,137e,511b,116a} from the Fertile N ≡ P (mod 18) are in the series.
Notice that all P ∊ {2a,8b,11c,17d,20e,26f} from the TV-classes N ≡ PTV (mod 33) are in the series.
This implies that all P-values in above shown classes N ≡ P (mod A) are in fact connected to †1j.

12 of 66

It is observed in the Flowcharts 3a-f that all the TV-classes now have higher modulo ATV = 33,
and that it is now possible to determine the congruence modulo 3 in more of the odd classes
connecting to Fertile branches, as all the IV-classes from Flowchart 2a & 2b have now been
separated into the three types { 1 , 2 , 3 }. This would also be possible for other Side-branches
on the horizontal Exit-branches containing subsets of the classes from Flowchart 2a & 2b.
Notice that from the six Flowcharts 3a-3f, it can be deduced exactly which subsets of the
Side-branches on the two Main-branches in Flowchart 2a & 2b, that are Fertile (have Sidebranches) and the one of three Infertile Side-branches (which do not have any Side-branches).
While it is definitely true that more information is gained by tripling the modulo, it seems to
come at some kind of cost, as a "loss of generality" is observed concerning the *Converging
IV-classes. For example for the *reducing IV-class *N ≡ 3 (mod 16) subsets are now observed
in several charts; N ≡ 19 (mod 25 ) & N ≡ 3 (mod 26 ) & N ≡ 35 (mod 27 ) & N ≡ 99 (mod 28 )
Observations in the shown Flowcharts 2a & 2b and Flowcharts 3a-f leads to the following:
Lemma 2
When Side-branches on Fertile branches are observed, any three consecutive
branches are a triple of one each of the three types of branches { 1 , 2 , 3 }.
Proof for Lemma 2
It is observed that an odd value (2C-1) in a Side-branch have the connection-point (6C-2) in
the Parent-branch. For the next Side-branch on the Parent-branch it is true that modulo
have been doubled twice ergo the next connection-point is 4(6C-2) = (24C-8) and the odd
value for this point is (2C'-1) = ((24C-8)-1)/3 = (24C-9)/3 = (8C-3) = (8C-4)+1 = 4(2C-1)+1
Suppose N = (2C-1) = (3X-a), a < 3, implying 4N+1 = 4(3X-a)+1 = (12X+1)-4a
a = 0 ⇒ 4a ≡ 0 (mod 3) ∧ a = 1 ⇒ 4a ≡ 1 (mod 3) ∧ a = 2 ⇒ 4a ≡ 2 (mod 3) so the
(12X+1) ≡ 1 (mod 3) - but not the 4a - changes the congruence i.e. it is a cycle ∎
Comment
Remember that it is possible to extrapolate against type ←E operations (in the direction "UP")
without any limitation in the Flowcharts and all Class-Branches contains an infinity of nodes.
Suppose a Flowchart 2a and a Flowchart 2b are printed for all X ∊ ℕ. In this case each of the
printed Flowcharts shows the connections and relative positions between the Value-Branches
included, as each node now contain one value NX = (2r3sX-B) for a particular X. The infinity of
printed Flowcharts is a collection which includes all odd NX ∊ [2X-1] and all even NX ∊ [6X-2].
If all the six different Flowchart 3a to 3f are printed for all X ∊ ℕ, then the result is the same
infinite collection of Flowcharts for Value-Branches where iterationseries for N are observed.
Imagine treating the collection of printed Flowcharts as a jigsaw-puzzle, where the odd value,
N = (2C-1), in a specific Value-Branch in a particular Flowchart can be observed to overlap
with an odd value in another Flowchart. Remember that all the odd NX ∊ [6X-5] ⋃ NX ∊ [6X-1]
are also an element in one of the Side-branches found in the Lists of Side-branches (page 11).
When it is proved that all the printed Flowcharts in the collection are connected to all
the other Flowcharts in the collection, then The Collatz Conjecture will be proven true. If all
the Flowcharts are connected, they are actually part of one giant Flowchart, which includes all
odd N = (2C-1) implicitly all N = (2C-1)2r, C ∊ ℕ, r ∊ ℕ0, i.e. all natural numbers N ∊ ℕ.

†

A unique †End Class-series for NN ∊ ℕ have a unique End †parity-sequence: † R ... - E - ... - E - D
containing sd pcs of O Operation i.e. sd pcs of O E -tiles and d pcs of E Operation i.e. (d-sd) E -tiles.

The Collatz-constant Zeta
In *Converging *Class-series *u[2*rsX-*BCon]q →→ [3sX-Bj]TV it is true that; ½ ≤ 3s/2*rs < 1
For example the *reducing *Class-series *3[2*42X-*13Con]1 →→ [32X-76]TV ⇒ PIV = 3IV ∧ *26 = *PTV
where the ratio ATV/AIV = 32/2*42 is 0.5625 i.e. it is true for the *Class-series that ½ < 32/2*42 < 1
In †End-Class-series †[2dX-(2d-NN)End]N →→ [3sdX-(3sd-1)j]TV, d ∊ ℕ0, sd ∊ ℕ0; ½ < NN3sd/2d ≤ 1
For example in the †End-Class-series †[25X-29End]3 →→ [32dX-87]TV ⇒ PIV = 3IV ∧ †17 = †PTV
where the ratio ATV/AIV = 32d/25 is 0.28125 ⇒ 3IV32d/25 = 0.84375 i.e. ½ < 3332d/25 < 1 is true.
Notice that †End-IV-classes are proper subsets of *reducing IV-classes & BEnd ≡ *BCon (mod 2*rs).
OBS! From the iterationseries for N 0 = 7 (p. 12) it can be deduced that 7 belongs to these Classseries: *6[2*74X-*(27-70)Con]1 →→ [34X-(34-*511)]TV and †[211X-(211-77)End]7 →→ [35dX-(35d-1)16]TV
†
R O E-O E-O E-E-O E-E-E-O E-E-E-E-D
Theorem 2
For s any number of Type O Operation there exist a *reducing number *rs of Type E Operation
Proof for Theorem 2
The proof comes from observations of *Class-series for *Converging Initial Value classes:
*u[2*rsX-*BCon]q > [3sX-Bj=s+*r] where it is true that 2*rs > 3s i.e. that

3s/2*rs < 1

Remember that A = (P+B) which is true for all the IV-classes 2r = (PIV+B0) and for all TV-classes
3s = (PTV+Bj=s+r) so when ATV = AIV3s/2*rs it is true that (PTV+Bj=s+*r) = (PIV+*BCon)3s/2*rs which
implies PTV ≈ PIV3s/2*rs ∧ Bj=s+*r ≈ *BCon3s/2*rs and this delivers the desired criterion for
existence of *Converging IV-classes *u[2*rsX-*BCon]q which is that 2*rs-1 < 3s < 2*rs is true for s ∊ ℕ
Suppose that c ∊ ℝ and that s ∊ ℕ is number of type Odd-operations s = # O :
2c = 3s

⇔

log(2c) = log(3s)

⇔ clog2 = slog3

⇔ c = slog3/log2

In this case 2⌈c⌉-1 < 3s < 2⌈c⌉ is true when *rs = ⌈c⌉ is true, i.e. 2*rs-1 < 3s < 2*rs is true.
NB! Here ⌈c⌉ is the ceiling-function (round up to nearest integer) e.g. ⌈15.84⌉ = 16.
Notice the *Reducing Combinations (s,*rs) in 30 < 2*1 < 31 < 2*2 < 23 < 32 < 2*4 < 33 < 2*5 < 26 < ...
which is "A Never Ending Sequence" so *u[2*rsX-*BCon]q exist when *rs = ⌈s log3/log2⌉ is true ∎
OBS! If 2*r-1 < 3s < 2*r is true then 2*r < 3s+1 < 2*r+2 is true and sometimes 2*r < 3s+1 < 2*r+1 is true.
Corollary 3
The estimate PTV ≈ PIV3s/2*rs is a little to low and v.v. the estimate Bj=s+*r ≈ BCon3s/2*rs is a little
to high ⇒ Bj=s+r < B0 ⇔ reducing Combination (s,r) & Bj=s+r ≥ B0 ⇔ Diverging Combination
Definition 2
The ratio log3/log2 is named The Collatz-constant Zeta Z = log3/log2 ⇒ *rs = ⌈sZ⌉, s ∊ ℕ
The equation *rs = ⌈sZ⌉, s ∊ ℕ, Z = log3/log2 is named Formula (1) for future reference.
Please notice that; Z is irrational, which ensures that sZ is never an integer for any s ∊ ℕ.
Please notice that; Z = log103/log102 = lne3/lne2 = log23/log22 = log23 = 1.5849625007...
NB! The ratio log3/log2 is well-known in relation to parity-sequences for congruence-classes
e.g. R. Terras[III] uses the ratio to calculate densities for Diverging parity-sequences for # O → ∞.
Formulas shown in the present work are based on the same method i.e. delivers identical results.
17 of 66

Corollary 4
From Definition 2 follows, that it is possible to split all r ∊ ℕ0 into the two proper subsets:
*rs ∊ {10,21,42,53, ...} where *rs = ⌈sZ⌉ is true & ϕrv ∊ {00,31,62,93, ...} where ϕrv ≠ ⌈sZ⌉ is true.
Comment
The Total number of IV-classes for constant modulo 2r in [2rX-B0]IV is the Sum
of the three different types of IV-classes; ∣ϕDiv(r)∣ + ∣*Con(r)∣ + ∣ΔRed(r)∣ = 2r
Notice that ∣*Con(ϕrv )∣ = 0 & ∣*Con(*rs)∣ > 0. The Group is named G(s,*r) = ∣*Con(*rs)∣
Consequences of splitting IV-classes
All classes in the used model have the form [2r3sX-Bj] and for TV-classes parity depends on X.
All (Parent-)classes [2r3sX-Bj] are split into two Child-classes [2r3s(2X-1)-Bj] ⋃ [2r3s(2X)-Bj]
[2r3s(2X-1)-Bj] ⇔ [2r+13sX-(2r3s+Bj)j]

and

[2r3s(2X)-Bj] ⇔ [2r+13sX-Bj]

All

[2r(2X-1)-B0]

⇔ [2r+1X-(2r+B0)0]

and

[2r(2X)-B0]

⇔ [2r+1X-B0]

IV

[3s(2X-1)-Bj]

⇔ [213sX-(3s+Bj)j]

and

[3s(2X)-Bj]

⇔ [213sX-Bj]

TV

Notice that after splitting a TV-class it is always possible to perform operations on the two
new Child-classes, because the modulo is now even, but the Child-classes are extended with
different operations O E or E because [213sX-(3s+Bj)j] & [213sX-Bj] have opposite parity.
Notice that Children of [2rX-B0]IV are IV-classes, but Children of [3sX-Bj]TV are not TV-classes,
and that it is the P-value (and all odd X) in the Parent-class that gets the new higher B-value in
one Child-class, while it is N2 (and all even X) that keeps the same Bj in the other Child-class,
so it is the parity of Bj in TV-classes that decides the extensions OE/E of the parity-sequences.
Even PTV (i.e. Odd BTV) means the odd X in the Parent-class gets the E -tile extension (and v.v.).
It is a very important point, that either the P or the B is "lucky" and gets the E -tile extension.
When the modulo is doubled all the (Parent-)Class-series [2rXa-B0]IV →→ [3sXa-Bj]TV are split:
[2r+1Xb-B0]IV →→ [213sXb-Bj] (even Xa) and [2r+1Xc-(2r+B0)0] →→ [213sXc-(3s+Bj)j] (odd Xa)
For Odd Bj in the Parent-TV-class:
[2r+1Xb-B0]IV →→ [213sXb-Bj]

↗ [213s+1Xb-(3Bj-1)j+1] ↘ [3s+1Xb-((3Bj-1)/2)j+2]TV

[2r+1Xc-(2r+B0)0]IV →→ [213sXc-(3s+Bj)j] ↘ [3sXc-((3s+Bj)/2)j+1]TV ( E -tile for odd Xa & "lucky" P)

For Even Bj in the Parent-TV-class:
[2r+1Xb-B0]IV →→ [213sXb-Bj]

↘ [3sXb-(Bj/2)j+1]TV

( E -tile for even Xa & "lucky" B)

[2r+1Xc-(2r+B0)0]IV →→ [213sXc-(3s+Bj)j] ↗ [213s+1Xc-(3(3s+Bj)-1)j+1] ↘ [3s+1Xc-((3s+1-1+3Bj)/2)j+2]TV

Notice that when PTV is "very even" i.e. PTV = (2C-1)2r for a "high r", then P is "lucky" r times in
a row, while B is "unlucky" r times in a row, before the odd PTV = (2C-1) is reached. Similarly
when Bj is "very even" i.e. Bj = (2C-1)2r for a "high r", then B is "lucky" r times in a row, while P
is "unlucky" r times in a row, before the odd B j+r = (2C-1) is reached. Remember that every
time AIV is doubled from 2r to 2r+1, all the P ≤ 2r are paired with a new B-value; B = 2r+1-P, where
2r ≤ B < 2r+1, and all the B < 2r are paired with a new P-value; P = 2r+1-B, where 2r < P ≤ 2r+1.
Be aware that the pair {P,B} for P+B = 2r share a parity-sequence with r pcs of tiles { O E , E }.
18 of 66

While the N-values (the P-values) follows the rules in the 3N plus 1 Problem,
the B-values are observed to follow the rules in the 3N minus 1 Problem:
• For r > 0 and Odd Bj;
Bj+1 = (3Bj-1) and Bj+2 = (3Bj-1)/2
• For r > 0 and Even Bj;
Bj+1 = (Bj/2)

The Collatz Tree as Flowchart
It is well known, that it is not possible to prove The Collatz Conjecture by extrapolating values
N ∊ ℕ from the Origo N = 1! and generate the Collatz-Tree illustrated in Figure 1a. The Tree
obviously contains a countable infinity of Branches (2C-1)2r, C ∊ ℕ, r ∊ ℕ0 but it is not obvious
that the Tree contains all possible Branches for all odd (2C-1) for all C ∊ ℕ. The below shown
Flowchart 6 is equivalent to the Tree in Figure 1a. The nodes in Flowchart 6 contains the
unique †End-IV-classes for the P-values in the IV-classes N ≡ P (mod 2d) ⇒ P ∊ †[2dX-(2d-PP)End]P

Flowchart 6. The †End-IV-classes †[2dX-(2d-NN)End]N incl. †[20X-0j=0]N=1 for The Origo 1!
Summary
An iterationseries exist for all Initial Values Nj=0 ∊ ℕ; N0 → N1 → ... → Nj=s+r → ...
If Collatz Conjecture is true all iterationseries contains a *Reduced Value and the End-value 1j.
N0 → N1 → ... E → *N*j=s+*r → Nj=s+*r+1 → ... E → 2j=s+d-1 E → 1j=s+d
A Class-series exist for all Initial Value-classes [2rX-Bj=0]IV, B0 < 2r; [2rX-B0]IV → ... → [3sX-Bj=s+r]TV
If Collatz Conjecture is true all N ∊ ℕ belongs to a *Converging Class-series.
*u[2*rsX-*BCon]q →→ [3sX-Bj=s+*r]TV for a unique u ∊ ℕ, *rs = *⌈sZ⌉, s ∊ ℕ, X ∊ ℕ, *BCon ∊ ℕ0.
Notice that a *Converging IV-class *u[2*rsX-*BCon]q contains the pair u(Pq+*BCon) = 2*rs. The pair
implies two modular sets *N ≡ Pq (mod 2*rs) and ∆BRedundant ≡ *BCon (mod 2*rs) for all 2r > 2*rs.
Please notice that any subset ∆[2rX-∆BRedundant]IV of *u[2*rsX-*BCon]q is ∆Redundant for all r > *rs.
If Collatz Conjecture is true all NN ∊ ℕ belongs to a unique †End Class-series.
[2dX-(2d-NN)End]N →→ [3sdX-(3sd-†1)j=s +d]TV , s ∊ ℕ, X = 1, BEnd = (2d-NN), PIV = NN, PTV = †1.

†

d

The End †parity-sequence for an †End IV-Class is unique - it is only valid for PIV = NN. Notice a
*reducing *parity-sequence for a *Converging IV-class is valid for all NX ∊ *u[2*rsX-*BCon]q, X ∊ ℕ.
NB! The following main-sections contains a deeper analysis of (*reducing) parity-sequences.

3. The Infinite Complete Binary Trees
The scope of this main-section is to show that all Class-series for IV-classes can be identified
with a unique parity-sequence R ⋯ E - T where the Total number of tiles is r from the
modulo in IV-classes 2r and the number of O E -tiles is s from the modulo in TV-classes 3s:
[2rX-Bj=0]IV →→ [3sX-Bj=s+r]TV r = #Totaltile = # E Operation s = # O E tile = # O Operation r-s = # E tile

The Domino-Tree
An Infinite Binary Domino-Tree { O E Left , E Right , R Root} is defined using O E -tiles & E -tiles:
The Root-node R is in layer r = 0 and any node/tile is linked to two nodes/tiles in layer r+1
• The Left-child is an O E -tile & The Right-child is an E -tile

The Base-2 Tree
An Infinite Binary Base-2 Tree { 1 Left , 0 Right , P Root} containing binary strings is defined:
The Root-node P (the "head") is in layer r = 0 & all nodes have two Children in layer r+1
The Left-child have a 1 added between P and the current binary string (the "tail")
The Right-child have a 0 added between P and the current binary string

Graph 7. Base-2 sub-tree for the ϕDiverging Domain-class ϕ[22X-10]IV in layer r = 2.
Lemma 3
Any node in The Domino-Tree can be identified with a unique node in The Base-2 Tree.
Any node in The Base-2 Tree can be identified with a unique node in The Domino-Tree.
Ergo there exist a one-to-one-correspondence between IV-classes and parity-sequences.
Proof for Lemma 3
The proof follows from The Pigeon-hole Principle and the fact that in both Trees each of the
layers r = k contains 2k different nodes. In The Domino-Tree is found 2k different routes/
parity-sequences and in The Base-2 Tree is found 2k different [2kX-B0] with different B0 < 2k ∎

Comment
After pruning all *u[2*rsX-*BCon]q, u ∊ ℕ the Tree contains a countable infinity of *reducing routes.
Any sub-tree (which have not been pruned) in the Tree contains an Uncountable Infinity of
different routes/nodes. This implies, that only after pruning all *u→∞[2*rsX-*BCon]q→∞, u ∊ ℕ, is it
possible for the Tree to contain less than an Uncountable Infinity of different routes. Please
notice that the *Converging *parity-sequences are extensions of ϕDiverging ϕparity-sequences.
In the following sections in the present work it is shown, that there exist a countable infinity of
the *reducing *parity-sequences i.e. a countable infinity of *Converging IV-classes, and it is also
shown, that there exist a countable infinity of ϕDiverging IV-classes/ϕparity-sequences/routes.

The Reverse Fibonacci Tree
Suppose that Graph 10 is the starting-point for a Fibonacci Tree defined as an IN-Tree. In
this case all { O Left , E Right , D Root} can be associated with a Value N ∊ ℕ and an †End-IV-class.
The new Root-tile D have the Value N = 1 (The Origo 1!) and as 1 is Odd it is necessary to
prune the odd-odd-link O ↔ D disconnecting the entire left half of Graph 10 to get Graph 13.
Any node/sub-tree that can not be identified with Values N ∊ ℕ are pruned away i.e.
All links to the Left-child O are deleted for all N ≡ 0 (mod 6) and all N ≡ 2 (mod 6)
Also pruned is the Left-child for the E -node with the Value N = 4 (to avoid The Loop)

Comment
After pruning all [6X-0] ⋃ [6X-4] the Reverse Tree have a countable infinity of nodes N ∊ ℕ.

that all [6X-3] are included in the Tree, because all [6X-0] have been pruned, and that all [6X-2]

4. Possible *Converging *Conu(s,*r)q and possible ϕDiverging ϕDivu'(s,r)q'
The scope of this main-section is to show how analysis of parity-sequences leads to the valid
Formulas for the Densities of the three types [2rX-B0]IV {ϕDiverging, *Converging, ΔRedundant}
Arranging *Converging *Conu(*rs)q by *Reducing Combinations (s,*rs) and ascending PIV
All *Conu(*rs)q are arranged in Groups for constant s in (s,*⌈sZ⌉). The uindex u ∊ ℕ is used as a
counting variable for the unique *Converging IV-classes i.e. each unique pair u(PIV+*BIV) = 2*rs.
Inside Groups for constant (s,*rs) the indexq q ∊ ℕ is used to identify Pq < Pq+1 ⇒ qMax = ∣*Con(*rs)∣
The first *Converging IV-class *1[2*10X-*0Con]1 is associated with the index u = 1 & q = 1.
The second *Converging IV-class *2[2*21X-*3Con]1 is associated with the index u = 2 & q = 1, etc.
Pq + *BCon = 2*rs & *Conu(*rs)q &

Type E-operation, as the unique *parity-sequence consists of s O E -tiles and (*rs-s) E -tiles.
s = # O E -tiles

*rs - s = *⌈sZ⌉ - s = ⌈(Z-1)s⌉ = # E -tiles (incl. the * E -tile)

Each Group (s,*rs) in the List contains the complete set of *reducing *parity-sequences which
have s = # O type Odd Operations and notice that Groups (s,*rs), s ≥ 2, in the List contains the
complete set of ϕDiverging ϕparity-sequences which have (s-1) type Odd Operations. These
ϕ
parity-sequences in List 2 have (s-1) O E -tiles & (r-s) colourless E -tiles; (s-1) ≤ r < ⌈(s-1)Z⌉

List 2*138. Next List

If the final E -tile is removed from all *reducing *parity-sequences in a collection for a constant
*Reducing Combination (s,*rs) what is left is a collection of longest possible ϕDiverging ϕparitysequences, as the ϕDiverging Combination ϕ(s,*rs-1) is observed i.e. there exist a one-to-onecorrespondence between the ϕDiverging IV-classes ϕu'(s,*rs-1)q' and the *Converging IV-classes
*u'(s,*rs)q'. Please notice that the mentioned longest possible ϕparity-sequences ϕ R ... E'- T for
the ϕDiverging IV-classes represents subsets of IV-classes identified with shorter ϕDiverging
ϕ
sequences and that the longest possible ϕparity-sequences are re-found in the last sub-group
(max E before last O E ) in the Next List for (s+1,*rs+1). As observed above there exist a 1-to-1correspondence for sequences/IV-classes *Conu'≥3(*rs)q' ↔ ϕDivu'≥3(rs-1)q' for (s-1) ≤ r < ⌈(s-1)Z⌉.

one of the parity-sequences for one of the Child-classes is extended with one E -tile and the

that the Combination (s,r) splits into (s,r+1) & (s+1,r+1) and this is in actual fact identical
to the definition of Pascal's Triangle i.e. there exist a Binomial-distribution of (s,r). The
following Figure is a (left-justified) Pascal's Triangle with *Reducing Combinations marked.

Resultlist 1. The number of different IV-classes for increasing *Reducing Combinations (s,*rs)
For sufficiently high modulo AIV = 2r it is true for the Union of IV-classes [2rX-B0]IV, B0 < 2r, that:
2r-*10/2r = 1/2 of the possible residue-classes for B0 < 2r can be associated with *1[2*10X-*0Con]1
2r-*21/2r = 1/4 of the possible residue-classes for B0 < 2r can be associated with *2[2*21X-*3Con]1
2r-*42/2r = 1/16 of the possible residue-classes for B0 < 2r can be associated with *3[2*42X-*13Con]1
2r-*53/2r = 1/32 of the possible residue-classes for B0 < 2r can be associated with *4[2*53X-*21Con]1
2r-*53/2r = 1/32 of the possible residue-classes for B0 < 2r can be associated with *5[2*53X-*9Con]2
2r-*74/2r = 1/128 of the possible residue-classes for B0 < 2r can be associated with *6[2*74X-*121Con]1
2r-*74/2r = 1/128 of the possible residue-classes for B0 < 2r can be associated with *7[2*74X-*113Con]2
2r-*74/2r = 1/128 of the possible residue-classes for B0 < 2r can be associated with *8[2*74X-*69Con]3
⋯

A density 1/2*rs can be found for all *u[2*rsX-*BCon]q, u ∊ ℕ, for all 2*rs ≤ 2r.
The Series-Formula
Definition 4
The result of Formula (3) is the Fraction of IV-classes [2rX- B0]IV, B0 < 2r, for a *Reducing
Combination (s,*rs) which are *reduced in exactly *j = s+*rs Operations Odd & Even.
F(s,*rs) = G(s,*rs)/2*rs

this Fraction (ratio) is a density

(3)

The result of Formula (4) is the Sum of the above Fractions i.e. of the subsets of IV-classes
*u[2*rsX-*BCon]q for constant (s,*rs) which are *reduced in at most *j = s+*rs Operations O & E

Notice that the Sum/Density S(s,*rs) is simultaneously an expression for the fraction of N ≤ 2*rs
which are *reduced in at most *j = (s+*rs) = (# O +*# E ) operations, and an expression for the
fraction of NX ∊ ϕ[20X-00]IV/TV (all N ∊ ℕ) which are *reduced in at most *j = (s+*rs) operations.
The Union of the 2*rs pcs of [2*rsX-B0]IV, 0 ≤ B0 < 2*rs, is a Covering System and all the IV-classes
have different parity-sequences - but with the same number of tiles; *rs = (# O E tile + # E tile)

In Resultlist 3 it is
observed for *rs → ∞ that:
2*rs/∣ΔRed(*rs)∣ is
strictly decreasing
2*rs/∣ϕDiv(*rs)∣ is
strictly increasing
2*rs/∣*Con(*rs)∣ is
increasing (not strictly)
"The Average Distance"
is higher for *Converging
than for ϕDiverging IVclasses for all *rs > 2.

Resultlist 3. The Average Distance between the IV-classes {Δ,ϕ,*}
Lemma 4
The Average Distance in the Union of elements NX ∊ ϕu'[2*rsX-B0]q' grows much faster than *rs
Proof for Lemma 4
In the individual ϕDiverging IV-classes the distance between neighbour-elements NX & NX+1 is
the modulo AIV = 2r and obviously 2r tends to infinity much faster than r ∊ ℕ for r → ∞. In
Resultlist 3 it can be observed that The Average Distance between the ϕDiverging IV-classes
2*rs/∣ϕDiv(*rs)∣ also tends to infinity much faster than *rs ∊ ℕ for *rs → ∞. That both the
distance between the possible IV-classes and the distance between individual elements in the
finite set of IV-classes for constant *rs tends to infinity much faster than *rs implies that the
distance between elements in the Union of ϕu'[2*rsX-B0]q' tends to infinity much faster than *rs ∎
Comment
In the extreme for "very high" r ∊ ℕ it is observed that the distance between elements in the
Union of ϕu'[2rX-B0]q' is "much higher". For the hypothetical "r close to ∞" it is observed that
the distance between elements in the Union of ϕu'[2rX-B0]q' tends to "equal to infinity" and for
"r equal to ∞" the distance apparently becomes "higher than infinity" i.e. non-existent. This is
an indication that a hypothetical counterexample Nce can not exist, as Nce would need to be
higher than any N ∊ ℕ, because all N ∊ ℕ belongs to an existing *[2*rsX-*BCon]IV for some *rs ∊ ℕ.
Please notice that the foundation for two important conclusions have been established:
• There exist a countable infinity of *Converging IV-classes *u[2*rsX-*BCon]q for u ∊ ℕ
• There exist a countable infinity of ϕDiverging IV-classes ϕu'[2rX-B0]q' for u' ∊ ℕ, u' ≥ 3
These infinities of IV-classes are subsets of the ϕDiverging Universe-class ϕ[20X-00]IV/TV
Notice that *Converging IV-classes (but not ϕDiverging IV-classes) are pairwise disjoint.

The Algorithm
Appendix Omega contains the code (Thonny-Python) for The Algorithm incl. some examples.
NB! It is strongly recommended to have a look at the two examples for N = 15 & N = (214+15)
as they illustrate some important properties about the pair (PIV+*BCon) = 2*rs in *[2*rsX-*BCon]IV

The *Converging IV-class *[2*rsX-*BCon]IV
NB! The Algorithm is not able to find the u & q
The corresponding TV-class in the Class-series *[2*rsX-*BCon]IV →→ [3sX-Bj=s+*rs]TV
The position X i the *reducing IV-class NX ∊ *[2*rsX-*BCon]IV
NX = NN
The *Reducing Combination (s,*rs) & The Ending Combination (sd,d)
The End-IV-class †[2dX-BEnd]N →→ [3sdX-Bj=sd+d] BEnd = (2d-NN ) & Bj=s +d = (3sd-†1j )

•

The *reducing iterationseries N0 → ... → *N*j=s+*rs < N0 for Nj in Base-10 and in Base-2

The shared *reducing *parity-sequence * R O E - O E -...- E - T for *[2*rsX-*BCon]IV, X ∊ ℕ

Also included is an Algorithm to Test B-values. Here rules from the 3N-1 Problem are used.
This Algorithm is able to find the *reducing IV-class associated with the Test-B and also print
the *reducing *parity-sequence for *[2*rsX-*BCon]IV. All odd B except {1,5,17} are reduced at some
point, so all B-values eventually gets caught in a B-loop (see the Discussion for more details).
Note
The keen and/or curious reader is highly encouraged to copy the code on the final pages of
Appendix Omega and test different odd N0 and B0. NB! On a normal laptop the code/ The
Algorithm is accurate for N < 1013 (but would have "PERFECT accuracy" on a "PERFECT computer").
Examples (from The Algorithm) of high P versus high B in the *Converging IV-classes:

*P = 2^r-*B = 999 *P in binary: 1111100111

Remember that in IV-classes the pair (PIV+BIV) = AIV = 2r is observed so when the focus is on the
binary strings for *P & *B in the *reducing IV-classes, it is observed that the "head" for one of
the values {*P,*B} have a sequence of one's and the "head" for the other one of the values
{*B,*P} have a sequence of zero's in the string and notice that the strings "fits like a zipper"
because the last 1 in the "tail" (for 20) have to carry all the way to the "head" 2*rs = *PIV + *BCon

5. Discussion
The scope of this last main-section is (from the previous shown Figures and argumentation)
to extract all the clues for the explanation of The Governing Dynamics controlling The Problem.
The Governing Dynamics in The Problem is the result of two bijections
For ra > 0 in (2Ca-1)2r a Type Even Operation exist: (2Ca-1)2r ⥂E
For ra = 0 in (2Ca-1)2r a Type Odd Operation exist: (2Ca-1) ⥂O
a

(2Ca-1)2r -1
(2Cb-1)2r , (2Ca-1)≠ †1Loop

The Result of an Operation is absolutely determined, but it is possible for two different values
N (one odd and one even) to reach the same Result e.g. 5j ⥂O 16j+1 and 32j ⥂E 16j+1, so the
rules in The Problem - taken as a whole - is not a bijection while the rules individually are.

Conclusions from the Flowcharts
If a Flowchart 2a and a Flowchart 2b is printed for all X ∊ ℕ the individual Flowcharts now
contains Value-Branches as each node now have a distinct value N X = (2C-1)2r, C ∊ ℕ, r ∊ ℕ0,
where NX ∊ [2r3sX-B], B < 2r3s, X ∊ ℕ. Notice that the Domain-class ϕ[22X-10]IV is found in the
first Side-branch in Flowchart 2b, so it is guaranteed that each printed type 2 Flowchart for
a constant X ∊ ℕ contains a specific NX from {31,72,113,154,195,236,277, ... ,NX, ...} = ϕ[22X-10]IV.
Remember that it is the same infinity of Flowcharts (with Value-Branches) that is printed,
when Flowchart 2a&2b is printed for all X ∊ ℕ, Flowchart 3a&3b&3c&3d&3e&3f is printed
for all X, when 4a&4b&4c&4d is printed or when 5a&5b&5c&5d&5e&5f&5g&5h is printed.
When the modulo is doubled it is possible to print Flowcharts with extended parity-sequences.
Remember that;

*1[2*10X-*0Con]1 ⋃ *2[2*21X-*3Con]1 ⋃ ϕ[22X-10]IV = ϕ[20X-00]IV/TV = ℕ

All subsets ∆[2rX-∆B0]IV , ∆B0 ≡ *0Con (mod 21), r > *10, of *1[2*10X-*0Con]1 are ∆Redundant.
All subsets ∆[2rX-∆B0]IV , ∆B0 ≡ *3Con (mod 22), r > *21, of *2[2*21X-*3Con]1 are ∆Redundant.
For r > 2 then all *u[2*rsX-*BCon]q and all ϕ[2rX-B0]IV are subsets of the Domain-class ϕ[22X-10]IV.
If the focus is on *Converging subsets of the Domain-class it is possible to start with only one
Flowchart 2b, as the first Side-branch on the branch with the odd class [6X-1] is ϕ[22X-10]IV.
Every time the modulo is doubled an IV-class [2rX-B0]IV for B0 ≡ 1 (mod 22) is split into two
IV-classes; [2r+1X-(2r+B0)0]IV ⋃ [2r+1X-B0]IV = [2rX-B0]IV and the parity-sequences are extended.
It is possible to identify the *Converging subsets (u ≥ 3) of the Domain-class when it is
observed that the TV-class [3sX-Bj]TV have smaller modulo 3s than the IV-class *u[2*rsX-*BCon]q
in which case 2*rs > 3s & *BCon > B*j=s+*r & (2*rs -*BCon) > (3s -B*j=s+*r ) i.e. PIV > PTV.
s

s

In the extreme for r → ∞ the pile of Flowcharts for *Converging IV-classes tends to infinity,
u → ∞, and the pile of charts containing ϕDiverging IV-classes Covers less & less of N ∊ ℕ.
An alternative loop is not possible, because there exist only one flow-direction for any node in
any of the infinity of printed Flowcharts; the direction "DOWN" which is towards the Origo 1!

Conclusions from the Binary Trees and the analysis of parity-sequences
The most important property of The Domino-Tree is, that for all layers r the Union of the 2r
different IV-classes [2rX-B0]IV, B0 < 2r, is a Covering System i.e. all N ∊ ℕ are included in a class.
There exist an Uncountable Infinity of different routes/parity-sequences in The Domino-Tree
and of these a (comparatively small) countable infinity are *reducing and can be identified
with a *Converging Class-series *u[2*rsX-*BCon]IV > [3sX-Bj=s+*r ]TV, u ∊ ℕ. In the Domino-Tree
there also exist a countable infinity of †Convergent routes, which can be identified with End
Class-series †[2dX-(2d-NN)End]N >> [3sdX-(3sd-1)j=s +d]TV, N ∊ ℕ. From List 2 it is known, that for
any ϕDiverging parity-sequence there exist a *reducing extension having the same amount of
the increasing O E -tiles and also a *reducing extension having one more O E -tile. In all
ϕ
Diverging IV-classes NX ∊ ϕ[2kX-*B0]IV, k < *rs, there exist a countable infinity of *Converging
IV-classes and for all NX it is possible to find the †Convergent †End IV-class †[2dX-(2d-NN)End]N=N
s

d

X

Please notice in Resultlist 2 that for all (s,*rs), s → ∞; ∣*Con(*rs)∣ / ∣ϕDiv(*rs)∣ > ∣ϕDiv(*rs)∣/2*rs
The ratio for ∣*Con(*rs)∣ / ∣ϕDiv(*rs)∣ is more significant than the density for ϕDiv(*rs) i.e. a
larger fraction of ϕDiv(*rs) are *Converging than the fraction of the Total which are Diverging.

Observations about collections of Class-series
In the analysis leading to the present work all Class-series for all IV-classes [2rX-B0]IV, r ≤ 13
and B0 ≡ 1 (mod 4) were constructed. For constant r the Class-series including all B0 < 2r is a
complete collection as it contains all the possible subsets of The Domain-class ϕ[22X-10]IV.
Besides generating all the *reducing IV-classes listed in Appendix Alpha the collections for
constant r reveals, that (some) Class-series merge i.e. have identical Terminal Value-classes.
The first example is found in the collection for r = 9, where it is observed that the ΔClass-series
for Δ[29X-1210]IV & Δ[29X-1250]IV both terminates at the TV-class [34X-1913]TV. What this means
is, that the iterationseries for 387 & 391 reach the same N after 13 Operations, and it implies
that for all r ≥ 9 the ΔClass-series for Δ[2rX-1210]IV & Δ[2rX-1250]IV have identical [3sX-Bj=s+r]TV:
3870 ↗ 11621 ↘ 5812 ↗ 17443 ↘ 8724 ↘ 4365 ↘ *2186 ↘ 1097 ↗ 3288 ↘ 1649 ↘ 8210 ↘ 4111 ↗ 12412 ↘ 6213
3910 ↗ 11741 ↘ 5872 ↗ 17623 ↘ 8814 ↗ 26445 ↘ 13226 ↘ 6617 ↗ 19848 ↘ 9929 ↘ 49610 ↘ *24811 ↘ 12412 ↘ 6213

In the collections for higher r it is observed that more and more ΔClass-series merge.
The explanation for the merging ΔClass-series is, that the TV-classes "run out" of BTV < ATV.
After a type Odd operation the new value is Nj+1=3Nj+1
and it is observed, that Nj+1 ≡ 1 (mod 3) and also that
Nj+1 ≡ 1 (mod Nj). In a Class-series it is observed that
[2r3sX-Bj] ⥂O [2r3s+1X-(3Bj-1)j+1], (3Bj-1)j+1 ≡ 2 (mod 3)
so after the first type Odd operation all the B-values are
Bj ≡ 1 (mod 3) or Bj ≡ 2 (mod 3) i.e. only two of three
B-values lower than ATV = 3s are possible in the ΔClassseries for ΔRedundant Combinations (s,r), r > *rs.
Figure 3b for the Domain-class
Notice in Figure 3b that the coefficients to the left of the marked Totals are Redundant, as the
combinations (s,r) have fewer type Odd operations than necessary for a *reducing combination
(s,*rs) and the coefficients below the marked Totals are also Redundant, as the combinations
have more type Even operations than necessary for a *reducing combination (s,*rs) e.g. for the
combination (s,r) = (5,15) there exist 286 Class-series Δ[215X-B0]IV →→ [35X-B20]TV where all
the B20 are lower than ATV = 35 = 243 so some Class-series for different B0 do have identical B20.

40 of 66

Observations about well-ordered sets
All IV-classes are well-ordered in the sense that they contain a lowest element P = (2r-B0), so
the Union of *Converging IV-classes *u[2*rsX-*BCon]q≤q for a constant *reducing combination
(s,*rs) also contain a lowest element P*rs which is found in the IV-class for q = 1 as the classes
are arranged according to size of P1 < P2 < ... < Pq so P1 is the representative P*rs for the Group.
There exist a countable infinity of P*rs ∊ {2*10 , 1*21 , 3*42 , 11*53 , 7*74 , 39*85 , 287*106 , ...} for s → ∞.
Max

Max

For a constant *reducing combination (s,*rs) the Union of ϕDiverging IV-classes ϕ[2*rsX-ϕB0]IV
also contains a lowest element i.e. the lowest N which is not yet included in a *Converging IVclass having a modulo lower than or equal to the current modulo 2*rs. As the modulo grows
more and more of these Threshold-values NT ∊ {1*21 , 3*42 , 7*74 , 27*5937 , 703*8151 , ...} are found
to belong to the shown set of Group-representatives P*rs and in actual fact the set of NT is also
a countable infinity, as for any *reducing combination a lowest N exist in the Union of ϕDiv(*rs).
OBS! Appendix Omega contains more data about the Class-series for Threshold-values NT.

The lowest value PIV in a ϕDiverging IV-class can not be unlucky forever

Every time we go two layers UP in The DominoTree the four lowest NX in any IV-class [2rX-B0]IV
will be the lowest value P in one of the Child-IVclasses [2rX-B0]IV = [2r+2X-B0]IV ∪ [2r+2X-(2r+B0)0]IV
∪ [2r+2X-(2r+1+B0)0]IV ∪ [2r+2X-(2r+1+2r+B0)0]IV and
three of the parity-sequences are more reducing
with the extensions { E - E , E - O E , O E - E } and
only one of the four is less reducing { O E - O E }.

Figure 5. Only one of the four NX ≤ 4 in [2 X-B0]IV, AIV = 2r can be unlucky when AIV becomes 2r+2.
r

Any ϕDiverging Class-series ϕ[2kX-ϕB0]IV →→ [3sX-Bj=s+k]TV have a ϕDiverging ϕparity-sequence
consisting of s O E -tiles & (k-s) E -tiles and the lowest value in the IV-class is PIV = (2k-ϕB0).
The first layer r in The Domino-Tree containing a *reducing subset of ϕ[2kX-ϕB0]IV is *rs = ⌈sZ⌉
where the IV-class with a *parity-sequence extended only with the decreasing E -tiles is found
and as 2(*rs-k) subsets of ϕ[2kX-ϕB0]IV exist in layer *rs one of the lowest NX for X ≤ 2(*rs-k) is found
as the P = (2*rs-*BCon) in the *Converging IV-class *[2*rsX-*BCon]IV where *BCon ≡ ϕB0 (mod 2k). In
the next *reducing layer *rs+1 there exist (*rs-k) *reducing subsets of ϕ[2kX-ϕB0]IV and in all the
*reducing layers *r for higher modulo a fast-growing number of *reducing subsets exist. It is
inevitable that the lowest value in the ϕDiverging IV-class P = (2k-ϕB0) eventually reach a layer
for a modulo high enough for the parity-sequence to be *reducing. No restrictions on r ∊ ℕ exist.
But there do exist restrictions on how much higher than P it is possible for *rs to get, before the
P is shown to be an element in a *Converging IV-class e.g. for all P > 1 it is observed that *rs < 2P.
No N can visit the power-set of N (i.e. 2N) different layers/classes before they are *reduced. Not
enough ϕDiverging routes/classes are found in the Tree (not enough O E -tiles are available).

Observations about sifted sets
If *Converging *parity-sequences are pruned in The Domino-Tree (with rIV as layers) or in The
(OUT) Fibonacci-Tree (with jTV = sTV+rIV as layers) the *Converging IV-classes are in essence
sifting the set of natural numbers, and each *reducing layer "catches" all NX ∊ *Conu(*rs)q≤q .
When all *u[2*rsX-*BCon]q for all u ∊ ℕ from List 1 have been pruned no ϕDiverging subset is left.
OBS! An Uncountable Infinity of ΔRedundant routes/sub-classes is removed in each pruning.
Max

41 of 66

Final Comment
Notice that in TV-classes [3sX-Bj=s+r]TV it is the parity of Bj =s +r that decides if it is the odd or
the even X that are lucky and have the parity-sequence extended with an E -tile when the
classes are split as the modulo is doubled and which subset is unlucky and get the O E -tile:
• Even Bj=s+r
⇒ odd X (incl. X = 1) are unlucky because [213sX-(3s+Bj=s+r)j] is Odd
• Odd Bj=s+r
⇒ odd X (incl. X = 1) are lucky because [213sX-(3s+Bj=s+r)j] is Even
• The BTV is sometimes odd and sometimes even, so sometimes NX for odd X are lucky
NB! Remember that Bj-values in Class-series follows the rules in the 3N minus 1 problem.
As a growing number of *Converging IV-classes are confirmed, an equivalent number of
elements disappears from the Union of ϕDiverging IV-classes and "The Average distance"
between the individual ϕDiverging IV-classes i.e. 2r/∣ϕDiv(r)∣ tends to infinity for r → ∞.
Eventually "The Average distance" will be higher than any previous Total 2r for *rs >> r.
TV

TV

IV

End-IV-classes †[2dX-(2d-NN)End]N only depends on d = # E Operation so eventually d is high enough.

Final Theorem
The Collatz Conjecture can not be false, because it is impossible for a counterexample to exist.

Proof for the Final Theorem
•
•
•
•
•

Formula (1) (*reducing number of E-operations *rs = *⌈sZ⌉ for a given s = # O ) is exact
The Sequence-formula (number of *Converging IV-classes u(*rs)q for a given s) is exact
Formula (3) (density of *Converging IV-classes u(*rs)q for a given s) is exact
The Series-formula (Sum of densities for all *Conu(*rs)q lower or equal to s) is exact
That all the Formulas are exact implies that Formula (5) is also exact
Density of ϕDiverging IV-classes

∣ϕDiv(*rk)∣/2*rk =

∞

∑ F(s , ⨳rs)

Formula (5)

s=k+1

For all k ∊ ℕ the density of ϕDiverging IV-classes equals the Sum for *Converging IV-classes
which have higher modulo AIV = 2*rs than 2⌈kZ⌉ (a countable infinity of s > k exist for all k ∊ ℕ).
Suppose that in Figure 2 all *u[2*rsX-*BCon]q for all u ∊ ℕ are marked by colour. In this
case there is absolutely NO uncovered area left in Figure 2. For s → ∞ the Union of
*Converging IV-classes *u→∞[2*rsX-*BCon]q→∞ for all u ∊ ℕ is a Covering System. All the
*Converging IV-classes are pairwise disjoint, as they are proper subsets of ϕ[20X-00]IV/TV
For all N ∊ ℕ it is true for modulo 2*rs in the *Converging IV-class N ∊ *u[2*rsX-*BCon]q that the
exponent *rs can not be higher than 2 N i.e. not higher than the power-set of N. In reality it is
observed that *rs is much lower than 2N and amongst "low N" it is observed that *rs < 3N &
the value 27 = 33 represents a local maximum as 27 ∊ *u[2*5937X-*(259-27)Con]q=1, 59/27 = 2.185...
For any k ∊ ℕ the Union of ϕDiverging IV-classes ϕ[2*rkX-B0]IV contain a lowest element, as all
classes are well-ordered and for a combination (k,*rk) a finite number of ϕ[2*rkX-B0]IV exist.
At some point for a higher modulo the lowest element is shown to belong to a *u[2*rsX-*BCon]q:
*NX ≡ Pq (mod 2*rs) ⇔ ∆B0 ≡ *BCon (mod 2*rs) ⇒ Union of *Converging NX ∊ *u→∞[2*rsX-*BCon]q → ℕ
A hypothetical lowest counterexample 2rce-1 < Nce < 2rce can not exist
which implies that The Collatz Conjecture can not be false! QED!
42 of 66

Suggestions for future work

The method used in the present work can also be used on the QN+1 Problem for other Q > 3:
•
•

For example for Q = 5 it is simple to prove that more than one N-loop exist:
10
↗ 61 ↘ 32 ↗ 163 ↘ 84 ↘ 45 ↘ 26 ↘ 17
130
↗ 661 ↘ 332 ↗ 1663 ↘ 834 ↗ 4165 ↘ 2086 ↘ 1047 ↘ 528 ↘ 269 ↘ 1310

The higher the Q - the lower is the Sum of Fractions S(s,*⌈slogQ/log2⌉) for s → ∞.
It is only for Q = 3 (and the degenerate case for Q = 1) that it is possible for the Asymptotic
Density for the Sum of Fractions to have the limit 1; S(s,*⌈slog3/log2⌉) → 1 for s → ∞.
Notice; Z5 = log5/log2 = 2.3219...

Z7 = log7/log2 = 2.7475... Z9 = log32/log2 = 3.1699... etc.

In the end The Collatz Conjecture must be true because Z3 < 2, Z3 = log3/log2 = 1.5849625007...

The Test-value 15
N in binary: 1111
is an Element in the *reducing IV-class *[*AX-*B] where:
*A = 2^r = 128 *A in binary: 10000000
*B = *A-*P = 113 *B in binary: 1110001
*P = 2^r-*B = 15 *P in binary: 0001111
The Initial Value-class [2^ 7 X - 113 ]
The Test-series for *[*AX-*B] Terminates at the TV-class [AX-B] where:
A = 3^s = 81 A in Binary: 1010001
B = A- P = 71 B in Binary: 1000111
P = 3^s- B = 10 P in Binary: 0001010
The TerminalValue-class [3^ 4 X - 71 ]
The Test-value 15 represents the X'th value in
the *reducing IV-class *[2^ 7 X - 113 ]
where X = (N+*B)/2^r = 1
and the *Reduced Value 10 represents the same X in
the TV-class [3^ 4 X - 71 ]
The *reducing IV-class *[2^rX-*B], *P = (2^r-*B),
meaning 15 is congruent to 15 (mod 2^ 7 )
share the *Reducing Combination (s,r) = ( 4 ,, 7 )
i.e. the *reducing parity-sequence *ROEOE...ET is a combination of
s = 4 OE-tiles and (r-s) = 3 E-tiles

15 belongs to the END IV-class [2^dX - (2^d-N)] = [2^ 12 X - 4081 ]

The above example shows the full capacity of The Algorithm. The next example do not show
the iterationseries from the *Reduced Value to the End Value N = 1 (print-lines deactivated).
Please notice that we find the same *reducing IV-class as in the above example and notice in
particular for the iterationseries that the ending of the binary strings are identical, as we find
"enough middle zeros" in the strings, to the effect that the "head" and the "tail" in the strings
can not interact before the iterationseries reaches the *Reduced Value after 11 Operations.

The entries in the above table matches pairs *BCon & P, as it is possible to find the *Converging
IV-classes *u[2*rsX-*BCon]q by analysing either the N-values or the B-values, B ∉ {1,5,17}.
The infinity of *Converging IV-classes for all u ∊ ℕ exist and the Union of *u[2*rsX-*BCon]q
contains all N ∊ ℕ. The Union of ∆BRedundant ≡ *BCon (mod 2*r ) contains all B ∊ ℕ0 except {1,5,17}.

Examples of *reducing IV-classes & End-IV-classes for certain Threshold-values NT:
We disregard NX ∊ *u=1[2*10X-*0Con]q=1 = {21,4,6,8, ...} & NX ∊ *u=2[2*21X-*3Con]q=1 = {11,5,9,13, ...}
We focus on (*reducing) subsets in The Domain-class NX ∊ ϕ[22X-10] = {31,7,11,15,19,23, ...}
The Test-value 3
N in binary: 11
is an Element in the *reducing IV-class *[*AX-*B] where:
*A = 2^r = 16 *A in binary: 10000
*B = *A-*P = 13 *B in binary: 1101
*P = 2^r-*B = 3 *P in binary: 0011
The Test-series [2^ 4 X - 13 ] — [3^ 2 X - 7 ]

The Test-value 3 reaches The End-value N = 1 in
#Odd + #Even = 2 + 5 = 7 Total-operations,
and to reach The Loop the parity-sequence then have a
UNIQUE combination of 2 OE-tiles & 3 E-tiles
3 belongs to the END IV-class [2^dX - (2^d-N)] =
[2^ 5 X - 29 ]

Data for the Threshold-value Nj=0 = 3T = P in the IV-class *u=3[2*42X-13Con]q=1 = {31 ,192 ,353 , ...}
NB! Brute force analysis show, that the lowest value not in a *Conu(*rs)q for *rs ≤ *42 is NT = 7
The Test-value 7
N in binary: 111
is an Element in the *reducing IV-class *[*AX-*B] where:
*A = 2^r = 128 *A in binary: 10000000
*B = *A-*P = 121 *B in binary: 1111001
*P = 2^r-*B = 7 *P in binary: 0000111
The Test-series [2^ 7 X - 121 ] — [3^ 4 X - 76 ]

The Test-value 7 reaches The End-value N = 1 in
#Odd + #Even = 5 + 11 = 16 Total-operations,
and to reach The Loop the parity-sequence then have a
UNIQUE combination of 5 OE-tiles & 6 E-tiles
7 belongs to the END IV-class [2^dX - (2^d-N)] =
[2^ 11 X - 2041 ]

Data for the Threshold-value Nj=0 = 7T = P in the IV-class *u=6[2*74X-121Con]q=1 = {71 ,1352 , ...}
Observe that 111 ∊ *u=4[2*53X-21Con]q=1 & 231 ∊ *u=5[2*53X-9Con]q=2 & 151 ∊ *u=7[2*74X-113Con]q=2
NB! Brute force analysis show, that the lowest value not in a *Conu(*rs)q for *rs ≤ *74 is NT = 27
The Test-value 27
N in binary: 11011
is an Element in the *reducing IV-class *[*AX-*B] where:
*A = 2^r = 576460752303423488
*B = *A-*P = 576460752303423461
*P = 2^r-*B =
27

#Odd + #Even = 41 + 70 = 111 Total-operations,

The following code/Algorithm can be used in Thonny-Python (Freeware):
NB! Be aware of line indentations if copying from pdf.
# This Algorithm uses the Rules in The Collatz Conjecture to test N-values
# Notice that in the print "*" is an Index and NOT "multiplication"
# All N congruent to 1 mod 4 are reduced in three operations, so
# the "interesting" Test-values are the N congruent to 3 mod 4
Reduce : int = 0
Stop : int = 0
j : int = 0
s : int = 0
r : int = 0
X : int = 0
Test = int(input("Please input an integer Test-value N: "))
print(" ")
Start : int = Test
print(" R

Value in Series: ",Test,"

In binary:", str(bin(Test))[2:],)

if Test == 1 :
print("")
print("The Algorithm stops at N = 1")
print("")
print("Please try again for a Test-value N > 1")
Reduce = 1
Stop = 1
if Test == 2 :
print("")
print("N = 2 reaches N = 1 in one type Even operation")
print("")
print("Please try again for a Test-value N > 2")
Reduce = 1
Stop = 1
if Test % 2 == 0 :
print("")
print("The Test-value ", Start)
print("is an Element in the *reducing IV-class *[2X-0]")
print("")
Reduce = 1
while Reduce == 0 :
if Test % 2 == 1 :
Test = int(3*Test + 1)
s=s+1
j=j+1
print(" O #Odd : ", s , "
if Test % 2 == 0 :
Test = int(Test / 2)
r=r+1
j=j+1
print(" E #Odd : ", s , "

# Rule for Odd Values
# If the two print-lines below are de-activated
# the *reducing iteration-series is not printed
# and only the results are shown
#Even : ", r ," #Total : ", j , " Value in Series: ", Test, "

In binary: " , str(bin(Test))[2:])

# Rule for Even Values

#Even : ", r ,"

#Total : ", j , "

Value in Series: ", Test, "

In binary: " , str(bin(Test))[2:])

if Test < Start :
# Reduced-criterion
print(" T
s = ",s,"
r = ", r,"
s+r = ", j, "
Reduced Value: ",Test )
print("")
print("The Test-value ", Start , " N in binary: ", str(bin(Start))[2:])
print("is an Element in the *reducing IV-class *[*AX-*B] where:")
print(" *A = 2^r = ", 2**r , " *A in binary: " , str(bin((2**r)))[2:])
print(" *B = *A-*P = ", 2**r - (Start % (2**r)) , " *B in binary: " , str(bin(2**r - (Start % (2**r))))[2:] )
print(" *P = 2^r-*B = ", Start % (2**r), " *P in binary: " , str(bin(Start % (2**r)))[2:])
print(" The InitialValue-class [2^",r,"X -",2**r - (Start % (2**r)),"]")
print("")
print(" The Test-series for *[*AX-*B] Terminates at the TV-class [AX-B] where:")
print(" A = 3^s = ", 3**s, " A in Binary: ", str(bin((3**s)))[2:] )
print(" B = A- P = ", 3**s - (Test % (3**s)), " B in Binary: ", str(bin(3**s - (Test % (3**s))))[2:])
print(" P = 3^s- B = ", Test % (3**s), " P in Binary: ", str(bin(Test % (3**s)))[2:])
print(" The TerminalValue-class [3^",s,"X -",3**s - (Test % (3**s)),"]")
print("")
X = int(X + int(Start + 2**r - (Start % (2**r)))/2**r)
print("The Test-value ", Start ," represents the X'th value in")
print("the *reducing IV-class *[2^",r,"X -",2**r - (Start % (2**r)),"]" )
print("where X = (N+*B)/2^r = ", X )
print("and the *Reduced Value ",Test," represents the same X in")
print("the TV-class [3^",s,"X -",3**s - (Test % (3**s)),"]")
print("")
print("The *reducing IV-class *[2^rX-*B], *P = (2^r-*B), ")
print("meaning",Start,"is congruent to", Start % (2**r), "(mod 2^",r,") ")
print("share the *Reducing Combination (s,r) = (",s,",",r,")")
print("i.e. the *reducing parity-sequence *ROEOE...ET is a combination of ")
print("s = ",s," OE-tiles and (r-s) = ", r-s," E-tiles")
print("")
Reduce = 1
while Stop == 0 : # The Algorithm continues until N = 1
if Test % 2 == 1 :
Test = int(3*Test + 1)
s=s+1
j=j+1
#print(" O #Odd : ", s , "
if Test % 2 == 0 :
Test = int(Test / 2)
